The Langlands dual group is SO(2n) (or PSO(2n) depending on conventions), whose center is isomorphic to the component group of BunSpin(2n). The Hitchin base for Spin(2n) coincides with that for SO(2n), given by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 4, …, 2n−2 and the Pfaffian of degree n. The spectral curve is a 2n‑fold cover for the vector representation and two 2n−1‑fold covers for the half‑spin representations. The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization at the critical level produces a center isomorphic to functions on the space of SO(2n)‑opers. The geometric Langlands correspondence for Spin(2n) requires a direct sum over the four (or four) components of BunSpin(2n) weighted by characters of the component group. The Whittaker category captures only the component containing the trivial bundle; the other components correspond to twists by the half‑spin characters. The categorical trace of Hecke functors involves the vector representation of dimension 2n and the two half‑spin representations of dimension 2n−1. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for Spin(2n)‑connections with wild ramification yields Stokes data in SO(2n); the enhanced ind‑sheaf formalism on the local system stack records the half‑spin monodromy. The Fourier–Mukai kernel along the Hitchin fibration is a twisted D‑module on the product of the two Hitchin moduli spaces, with the twist given by the Poincaré line bundle on the product of the Prym varieties associated to the spectral covers.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Exceptional Group E₆ and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence

The exceptional group E₆, of dimension 78 and rank 6, has Langlands dual ²E₆ (the quasi‑split form of E₆). The moduli stack BunE₆ of E₆‑bundles on a smooth projective curve X has component group π₀(BunE₆) ≅ ℤ/3ℤ, reflecting the center of the simply connected group. The Hitchin base for E₆ is the space of invariant polynomials on 𝔢₆ of degrees 2, 5, 6, 8, 9, 12; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 36(g−1). The spectral curve is a 27‑fold cover of X determined by the characteristic polynomial in the 27‑dimensional fundamental representation. The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter ℏ = 1/(k + h∨) with dual Coxeter number h∨ = 12 controls the deformation; the critical level corresponds to ℏ = 0. At the critical level the center of the completed universal enveloping algebra of the affine Kac–Moody algebra Ẽ₆ is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of E₆‑opers. The geometric Langlands equivalence for E₆ is expected to be an equivalence Dbhol(D‑mod(BunE₆)) ≃ QCoh(LocSys²E₆) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution on the local system side. The categorical trace of Hecke functors involves the 27‑dimensional representation and the 78‑dimensional adjoint representation. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E₆‑connections with irregular singularities introduces Stokes matrices in ²E₆; the enhanced ind‑sheaf structure on LocSys²E₆ encodes the wild monodromy. The component group ℤ/3ℤ grades the derived category, and the Whittaker category captures only the neutral component; the other two components are obtained by twisting with the two non‑trivial characters of the center.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Exceptional Group E₇ and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence

The exceptional group E₇, of dimension 133 and rank 7, is self‑dual up to a central quotient; its Langlands dual is the adjoint form E₇ad. The moduli stack BunE₇ of simply connected E₇‑bundles on a smooth projective curve X has component group π₀(BunE₇) ≅ ℤ/2ℤ, reflecting the center of order 2. The Hitchin base for E₇ is the space of invariant polynomials on 𝔢₇ of degrees 2, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 18; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 63(g−1). The spectral curve is a 56‑fold cover of X determined by the characteristic polynomial in the 56‑dimensional fundamental representation (the symplectic representation). The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter ℏ = 1/(k + h∨) with dual Coxeter number h∨ = 18 controls the deformation; the critical level corresponds to ℏ = 0. At the critical level the center of the completed universal enveloping algebra of the affine Kac–Moody algebra Ẽ₇ is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of E₇‑opers. The geometric Langlands equivalence for E₇ is expected to be an equivalence Dbhol(D‑mod(BunE₇)) ≃ QCoh(LocSysE₇ad) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution on the local system side. The categorical trace of Hecke functors involves the 56‑dimensional representation and the 133‑dimensional adjoint representation. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E₇‑connections with irregular singularities introduces Stokes matrices in E₇ad; the enhanced ind‑sheaf structure on LocSysE₇ad encodes the wild monodromy. The component group ℤ/2ℤ grades the derived category, and the Whittaker category captures only the neutral component; the other component is obtained by twisting with the non‑trivial character of the center.

  • Confusing the component group of BunG with the center of the dual group LG; they are dual but not canonically identified without a pinning.
  • Assuming the Whittaker category on BunG captures the whole derived category for non‑simply connected groups; it only captures the trivial topological component.
  • Treating the critical level quantization parameter ℏ as a root of unity; it is generic unless k + h∨ is a rational number with denominator dividing the order of the center.
  • Neglecting that the Hitchin fibers for non‑simply connected groups are disconnected, and that the spectral curves for different components may have different genera.
  • Misidentifying the Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter with the affine Kac–Moody level k; the relation is ℏ = 1/(k + h∨).
  • Overlooking that the irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for exceptional groups involves Stokes matrices valued in the dual group, not merely formal monodromy.
  • Assuming the geometric Satake equivalence for non‑simply connected groups is a simple restriction of the simply connected case; the monoidal structure acquires a non‑trivial associator from the component group.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group G₂ and the Categorical Geometric Langlands Correspondence via the Whittaker Category

The exceptional group G₂, of dimension 14 and rank 2, is both simply connected and adjoint; its center is trivial, so the moduli stack BunG₂ of G₂‑bundles on a smooth projective curve X is connected. The Hitchin base for G₂ is the space of invariant polynomials on the Lie algebra 𝔤₂, which are generated by homogeneous polynomials of degrees 2 and 6. Consequently the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension (2+6)(g−1) = 8(g−1). The spectral curve is a 7‑fold cover of X determined by the characteristic polynomial in the 7‑dimensional fundamental representation of G₂.

The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter is ℏ = 1/(k + h∨) with dual Coxeter number h∨ = 4. At the critical level ℏ = 0 the center of the completed universal enveloping algebra of the affine Kac–Moody algebra Ĝ₂ is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of G₂‑opers. The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for G₂ is expected to be an equivalence Dbhol(D‑mod(BunG₂)) ≃ QCoh(LocSysG₂) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution on the local system side. Because the center is trivial, the Whittaker category on BunG₂ coincides with the whole derived category; there is no grading by a component group. The Hecke functors are labeled by the two fundamental representations of dimensions 7 and 14, and their categorical traces involve the corresponding characters of the dual group G₂.

Recent work constructs the Whittaker category for G₂ by using the mirabolic subgroup of the loop group and shows that the resulting category is equivalent to the category of modules over the quantum group Uq(𝔤₂) at a root of unity determined by the level k. The equivalence is compatible with the braided monoidal structure coming from the geometric Satake correspondence for G₂. This provides a concrete realization of the categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for G₂ at non‑critical levels and gives a framework for computing conformal blocks of the G₂ WZW model.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group F₄ and the Quantum Geometric Langlands Correspondence at Non‑Critical Levels

The exceptional group F₄ has dimension 52, rank 4, and a trivial center. Its dual Coxeter number is h∨ = 9. The moduli stack BunF₄ of F₄‑bundles on a curve X is therefore connected. The Hitchin base for F₄ is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6, 8, and 12; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension (2+6+8+12)(g−1) = 28(g−1). The spectral curve is a 26‑fold cover of X arising from the 26‑dimensional fundamental representation (the minimal representation of F₄).

The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter is ℏ = 1/(k + 9). For non‑critical levels (k ≠ −9) the parameter ℏ is non‑zero and the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence predicts an equivalence between the derived category of holonomic D‑modules on BunF₄ twisted by ℏ and the category of quasi‑coherent sheaves on the moduli stack of F₄‑local systems equipped with a suitable quantum deformation. The quantum deformation is governed by the quantum group Uq(𝔣₄) with q = exp(πiℏ). The Hecke functors are labeled by the fundamental representations of dimensions 26, 52, 273, and 324; their categorical traces are computed using the quantum trace in the representation category of Uq(𝔣₄).

At non‑critical levels the center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at level k is smaller than at the critical level; it is generated by the Segal–Sugawara operators and the higher Sugawara operators associated to the invariant polynomials of degrees 6, 8, and 12. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence for F₄ is expected to be compatible with the braided monoidal structure coming from the geometric Satake equivalence for the quantum group Uq(𝔣₄). This compatibility has been verified for the minimal representation (dimension 26) by constructing the corresponding Hecke eigensheaves via the quantum Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction.

Worked Example: Computing the Hitchin Base Dimension for F₄
Let X be a smooth projective curve of genus g = 3. Determine the dimension of the generic Hitchin fiber for the group F₄.
The Hitchin base for F₄ is the space of invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6, 8, 12. The dimension of the Hitchin base is the sum of the dimensions of the spaces of sections of the corresponding line bundles on X. For a curve of genus g, the dimension of H⁰(X, KX⊗d) is (2d−1)(g−1) for d ≥ 2. Thus the total dimension is Σd∈{2,6,8,12} (2d−1)(g−1) = [(3)+(11)+(15)+(23)]·(g−1) = 52·(g−1). For g = 3, this equals 52·2 = 104. The generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of half this dimension, i.e., 52. Alternatively, using the formula (sum of degrees)(g−1) = (2+6+8+12)(g−1) = 28·2 = 56. The discrepancy arises because the first formula counts the dimension of the base, while the fiber dimension is half the base dimension for a completely integrable system. The correct fiber dimension is 56.
Exam Strategy: Remembering Dual Coxeter Numbers
The dual Coxeter numbers for the exceptional groups are: G₂ = 4, F₄ = 9, E₆ = 12, E₇ = 18, E₈ = 30. Memorize these together with the dimensions of the minimal representations (7, 26, 27, 56, 248) and the degrees of the fundamental invariants. They appear repeatedly in quantization parameters, Hitchin base dimensions, and the critical level condition k + h∨ = 0.

D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group E₆ and the Derived Riemann‑Hilbert Correspondence

The simply connected group E₆ has dimension 78, rank 6, and center of order 3. Its Langlands dual is the adjoint group E₆ad. The moduli stack BunE₆ has component group π₀(BunE₆) ≅ ℤ/3ℤ. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 5, 6, 8, 9, 12; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 36(g−1). The spectral curve is a 27‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 27‑dimensional fundamental representation.

The derived Riemann‑Hilbert correspondence for E₆ relates the derived category of holonomic D‑modules on BunE₆ to the derived category of constructible sheaves on the character variety LocSysE₆ad. Because the component group is ℤ/3ℤ, the derived category splits into three blocks indexed by characters of the center. The Whittaker category captures only the neutral block; the other two blocks are obtained by twisting with the non‑trivial characters. The derived correspondence is an equivalence of ∞‑categories Dbhol(D‑mod(BunE₆)) ≃ Dbc(LocSysE₆ad) compatible with the six functors formalism. The irregular Riemann‑Hilbert correspondence for E₆‑connections with irregular singularities introduces Stokes matrices valued in the dual group E₆ad; the enhanced ind‑sheaf structure on LocSysE₆ad encodes the wild monodromy data.

At the critical level ℏ = 0 (k = −12) the center of the completed universal enveloping algebra of the affine Kac–Moody algebra Ẽ₆ is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of E₆‑opers. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence at the critical level is expected to be an equivalence between the derived category of D‑modules on BunE₆ and the category of quasi‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of E₆‑local systems, intertwining the Hecke action with convolution. The Hecke functors are labeled by the fundamental representations of dimensions 27, 78, 351, 2925, etc., and their categorical traces involve the corresponding characters of the dual group.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group E₇ and the Categorical Trace of Hecke Functors

The simply connected group E₇ has dimension 133, rank 7, and center of order 2. Its Langlands dual is the adjoint group E₇ad. The moduli stack BunE₇ has component group π₀(BunE₇) ≅ ℤ/2ℤ. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 18; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 63(g−1). The spectral curve is a 56‑fold cover of X determined by the characteristic polynomial in the 56‑dimensional symplectic representation.

The categorical trace of Hecke functors for E₇ is a central tool in the geometric Langlands program. For a Hecke functor HV associated to a representation V of the dual group E₇ad, the categorical trace is an endofunctor of the category Dbhol(D‑mod(BunE₇)) whose character is given by the trace of the corresponding Hecke operator on the space of conformal blocks. The most important representations are the 56‑dimensional fundamental representation and the 133‑dimensional adjoint representation. Their categorical traces satisfy a system of relations known as the Hecke relations, which are the geometric analogues of the Satake isomorphism.

Because the component group is ℤ/2ℤ, the derived category decomposes into two blocks. The Whittaker category corresponds to the trivial character; the other block is obtained by tensoring with the non‑trivial character of the center. The categorical trace respects this decomposition: the trace of HV on the non‑trivial block differs by a sign determined by the action of the center on V. For the 56‑dimensional representation the center acts trivially, so the trace is the same on both blocks; for the 133‑dimensional adjoint representation the center also acts trivially. However, for representations where the center acts non‑trivially (e.g., the 912‑dimensional representation), the trace acquires a sign on the non‑trivial block. This sign is crucial for the compatibility of the geometric Langlands equivalence with the component group grading.

D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group E₈ and the Wild Geometric Langlands Correspondence with Irregular Singularities

The exceptional group E₈ has dimension 248, rank 8, and a trivial center. It is self‑dual: the Langlands dual of E₈ is E₈ itself. The moduli stack BunE₈ is connected. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 8, 12, 14, 18, 20, 24, 30; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 124(g−1). The spectral curve is a 248‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the adjoint representation.

The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for E₈ studies D‑modules on the moduli stack of E₈‑connections with irregular singularities. The irregular Riemann‑Hilbert correspondence attaches to such a connection an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety, which is a moduli space of Stokes data valued in E₈. The Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of E₈, and their classification involves the root system of type E₈. The enhanced ind‑sheaf structure encodes the formal monodromy, the Stokes matrices, and the connection matrices between different sectors.

Because E₈ has no center, there is no component group grading, and the Whittaker category coincides with the whole derived category. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence for E₈ at the critical level (k = −30) is expected to be an equivalence Dbhol(D‑mod(BunE₈)) ≃ QCoh(LocSysE₈) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution. The Hecke functors are labeled by the fundamental representations of E₈, the smallest of which is the adjoint representation of dimension 248. The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the adjoint representation is closely related to the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the E₈ Hitchin system. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical levels involves the quantum group Uq(𝔢₈) with q = exp(πi/(k+30)). The representation theory of Uq(𝔢₈) at roots of unity controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory.

  • Confusing the component group of BunG with the center of the dual group LG; they are dual but not canonically identified without a pinning.
  • Assuming the Whittaker category on BunG captures the whole derived category for non‑simply connected groups; it only captures the trivial topological component.
  • Treating the critical level quantization parameter ℏ as a root of unity; it is generic unless k + h∨ is a rational number with denominator dividing the order of the center.
  • Neglecting that the Hitchin fibers for non‑simply connected groups are disconnected, and that the spectral curves for different components may have different genera.
  • Misidentifying the Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter with the affine Kac–Moody level k; the relation is ℏ = 1/(k + h∨).
  • Overlooking that the irregular Riemann‑Hilbert correspondence for exceptional groups involves Stokes matrices valued in the dual group, not merely formal monodromy.
  • Assuming the geometric Satake equivalence for non‑simply connected groups is a simple restriction of the simply connected case; the monoidal structure acquires a non‑trivial associator from the component group.

The Categorical Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Exceptional Group E6 via the Whittaker Category and the 27‑Dimensional Representation

The exceptional group E6 has dimension 78, rank 6, and a center isomorphic to ℤ/3ℤ. Its Langlands dual is again E6, but the duality exchanges the two 27‑dimensional fundamental representations (the minuscule weights ω₁ and ω₆). The moduli stack Bun E6 has three connected components indexed by the center ℤ/3ℤ. The Whittaker category Whitt(Bun E6) is defined as the full subcategory of D b hol (D‑mod(Bun E6)) consisting of objects with trivial monodromy along the fibers of the projection to the trivial component; it captures only the neutral component. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence at the critical level (k = −12) is expected to be an equivalence Whitt(Bun E6) ≃ QCoh(LocSys E6 )Wh, where the right side is the category of quasi‑coherent sheaves on the stack of E6‑local systems with a Whittaker condition at a marked point. The Hecke functors H V are labeled by finite‑dimensional representations V of E6. The 27‑dimensional representation V₂₇ (with highest weight ω₁) and its dual V₂₇* (with highest weight ω₆) are minuscule: their weights form a single Weyl orbit. The categorical trace of H V₂₇ on Whitt(Bun E6) is computed by the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the E6 Hitchin system. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 5, 6, 8, 9, 12; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 39(g−1). The spectral curve is a 27‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 27‑dimensional representation. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical levels involves the quantum group U q (𝔢₆) with q = exp(πi/(k+12)). At roots of unity, the representation theory of U q (𝔢₆) controls the fusion rules of the corresponding W‑algebra. The component group ℤ/3ℤ acts on D b hol (D‑mod(Bun E6)) by tensoring with the three characters. The Whittaker category corresponds to the trivial character; the other two blocks are obtained by tensoring with the non‑trivial characters. The categorical trace of H V₂₇ on the non‑trivial blocks differs by a cube root of unity determined by the action of the center on V₂₇. Since the center acts on V₂₇ by a primitive cube root of unity, the trace acquires a phase on the non‑trivial blocks. This phase is crucial for the compatibility of the geometric Langlands equivalence with the component group grading. The enhanced ind‑sheaf structure on the wild character variety for E6‑connections with irregular singularities encodes Stokes matrices valued in the maximal unipotent subgroup of E6, whose root system has 72 roots. The formal monodromy, Stokes matrices, and connection matrices satisfy the wild character variety relations, which are the geometric analogues of the quantum group relations for U q (𝔢₆).

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group E7 and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence at Non‑Critical Levels

The exceptional group E7 has dimension 133, rank 7, and a center isomorphic to ℤ/2ℤ. Its Langlands dual is again E7. The moduli stack Bun E7 has two connected components indexed by the center ℤ/2ℤ. The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical levels (k ≠ −18) involves the quantum group U q (𝔢₇) with q = exp(πi/(k+18)). The level k is related to the quantization parameter ℏ by ℏ = 1/(k+18). The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization of the Hitchin system produces a sheaf of algebras D ℏ on the moduli stack of Higgs bundles; its fiber at ℏ = 0 is the structure sheaf of the Hitchin base. The category of D‑modules on Bun E7 twisted by the line bundle corresponding to level k is equivalent to the category of modules over the affine Kac–Moody algebra 𝔢̂₇ at level k. The Verma modules M(λ) for 𝔢̂₇ at level k correspond to D‑modules on Bun E7 supported on the nilpotent cone. The Jantzen filtration on Verma modules translates to the weight filtration on the corresponding D‑modules. The Kazhdan–Lusztig polynomials for the affine Weyl group of type E7 control the composition multiplicities of Verma modules and the stalks of intersection cohomology complexes on the affine flag variety. The quantum geometric Langlands equivalence at non‑critical levels is expected to be an equivalence D b hol (D‑mod(Bun E7 )_k) ≃ QCoh(LocSys E7 )_q intertwining the Hecke action with convolution, where q = exp(πiℏ). The Hecke functors are labeled by the fundamental representations of E7. The smallest fundamental representation is the 56‑dimensional representation V₅₆ (with highest weight ω₇), which is symplectic and minuscule. The categorical trace of H V₅₆ is closely related to the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the E7 Hitchin system. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 18; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 66(g−1). The spectral curve is a 56‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 56‑dimensional representation. Because the center is ℤ/2ℤ, the derived category D b hol (D‑mod(Bun E7 )_k) decomposes into two blocks. The Whittaker category corresponds to the trivial character; the other block is obtained by tensoring with the non‑trivial character of the center. The categorical trace of H V₅₆ on the non‑trivial block differs by a sign determined by the action of the center on V₅₆. Since the center acts trivially on V₅₆, the trace is the same on both blocks. However, for representations where the center acts non‑trivially (e.g., the 912‑dimensional representation with highest weight ω₁), the trace acquires a sign on the non‑trivial block. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E7‑connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety, whose Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of E7. The root system of type E7 has 126 roots. The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for E7 at non‑critical levels involves the quantum group U q (𝔢₇) at roots of unity, whose representation theory controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory.

Examiner's Trap: Component Group vs. Center Duality
A question states: "For the exceptional group E7, the component group of Bun E7 is ℤ/2ℤ and the center of the Langlands dual group is also ℤ/2ℤ. Explain why these are not canonically identified without a pinning, and describe the effect on the categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the 912‑dimensional representation."
The component group π₀(Bun E7) ≅ ℤ/2ℤ classifies topological types of E7‑bundles. The center Z(E7) ≅ ℤ/2ℤ acts on representations of E7. A pinning (choice of Borel, maximal torus, and root vectors) gives an isomorphism π₀(Bun E7) ≅ Z(E7)^∨, but this isomorphism depends on the pinning. Without a pinning, there is no canonical identification. The 912‑dimensional representation V₉₁₂ has highest weight ω₁; the center acts on it by the non‑trivial character. Under the geometric Langlands equivalence, the component group grading on D b hol (D‑mod(Bun E7)) corresponds to the Z(E7)‑grading on QCoh(LocSys E7). The categorical trace of H V₉₁₂ on the non‑trivial component of Bun E7 differs by a sign from the trace on the trivial component because the center acts non‑trivially on V₉₁₂. If one incorrectly identifies the component group with the center without a pinning, the sign assignment becomes ambiguous, breaking compatibility with the Hecke relations.
Exam‑Day Strategy
When a question asks about the component group of Bun G for a non‑simply connected group G, always state: (1) π₀(Bun G) ≅ π₁(G) ≅ Z(L G), (2) the isomorphism requires a pinning, (3) the Whittaker category captures only the trivial component, (4) the categorical trace of a Hecke functor H V on non‑trivial components acquires a phase equal to the character by which Z(L G) acts on V. Memorize the centers and fundamental representations for E6 (ℤ/3ℤ, 27‑dim), E7 (ℤ/2ℤ, 56‑dim), E8 (trivial, 248‑dim), F4 (trivial, 26‑dim), G2 (trivial, 7‑dim).

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Exceptional Group E8 and the Quantum Geometric Langlands Correspondence

The exceptional group E8 has dimension 248, rank 8, and a trivial center. It is self‑dual: the Langlands dual of E8 is E8 itself. The moduli stack Bun E8 is connected. The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence at the critical level (k = −30) is expected to be an equivalence D b hol (D‑mod(Bun E8)) ≃ QCoh(LocSys E8) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution. At non‑critical levels (k ≠ −30), the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence involves the quantum group U q (𝔢₈) with q = exp(πi/(k+30)). The quantization parameter ℏ = 1/(k+30) is generic unless k+30 is a rational number with denominator dividing the order of the center; since the center is trivial, ℏ is generic for all non‑critical levels. The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization of the E8 Hitchin system produces a sheaf of algebras D ℏ whose fiber at ℏ = 0 is the structure sheaf of the Hitchin base. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 8, 12, 14, 18, 20, 24, 30; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 124(g−1). The spectral curve is a 248‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the adjoint representation. The Hecke functors are labeled by the fundamental representations of E8. The smallest fundamental representation is the adjoint representation V₂₄₈ (with highest weight ω₈). The categorical trace of H V₂₄₈ is closely related to the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the E8 Hitchin system. Because E8 has no center, there is no component group grading, and the Whittaker category coincides with the whole derived category D b hol (D‑mod(Bun E8)). The quantum group U q (𝔢₈) at roots of unity controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory. The representation theory of U q (𝔢₈) at a root of unity of order ℓ is semisimple for ℓ coprime to the Coxeter number 30. The modular tensor category of integrable representations of 𝔢̂₈ at level k corresponds to the category of D‑modules on Bun E8 twisted by the line bundle of level k. The Verlinde formula for E8 computes the dimension of the space of conformal blocks as a sum over the modular S‑matrix. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E8‑connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety, whose Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of E8. The root system of type E8 has 240 roots. The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for E8 at non‑critical levels involves the quantum group U q (𝔢₈) at roots of unity, whose representation theory is governed by the affine Weyl group of type E8. The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the adjoint representation satisfies the Hecke relations, which are the geometric analogues of the Satake isomorphism for the quantum group U q (𝔢₈). The quantum Satake isomorphism identifies the spherical Hecke algebra of U q (𝔢₈) with the algebra of functions on the quantum dual group, which is again E8.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group F4 and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence via the Theta Correspondence for the Short Root System

The exceptional group F4 has dimension 52, rank 4, and a trivial center. Its Langlands dual is again F4, but the duality exchanges the long and short roots. The root system of type F4 has 48 roots: 24 long roots forming a D4 subsystem and 24 short roots forming another D4 subsystem. The moduli stack Bun F4 is connected. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence at the critical level (k = −9) is expected to be an equivalence D b hol (D‑mod(Bun F4)) ≃ QCoh(LocSys F4) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution. The Hecke functors are labeled by the fundamental representations of F4. The fundamental representations have dimensions 26 (ω₁, the minimal representation), 52 (ω₂, the adjoint), 273 (ω₃), and 324 (ω₄). The 26‑dimensional representation is minuscule for the short root system: its weights are the 24 short roots plus 0 with multiplicity 2. The theta correspondence for F4 relates D‑modules on Bun F4 to D‑modules on the moduli stack of bundles for the short root subsystem D4. The short root subsystem D4 has a triality automorphism of order 3, which induces a symmetry on the category of D‑modules. The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the 26‑dimensional representation is computed by the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the F4 Hitchin system. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6, 8, 12; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 26(g−1). The spectral curve is a 26‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 26‑dimensional representation. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical levels involves the quantum group U q (𝔣₄) with q = exp(πi/(k+9)). The quantum group U q (𝔣₄) has a non‑trivial R‑matrix and a ribbon element. The representation theory of U q (𝔣₄) at roots of unity controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for F4‑connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety, whose Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of F4. The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for F4 studies the interplay between the long and short root systems under the Langlands duality. The theta correspondence for the short root system D4 ⊂ F4 induces a functor between the category of D‑modules on Bun F4 and the category of D‑modules on Bun D4, which intertwines the Hecke actions. The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the 26‑dimensional representation on the F4 side corresponds to the categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the vector representation of D4 on the D4 side, up to a normalization factor determined by the embedding index. The Verlinde formula for F4 computes the dimension of the space of conformal blocks as a sum over the modular S‑matrix of the affine Kac–Moody algebra 𝔣̂₄.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Group G2 and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence via the 7‑Dimensional Fundamental Representation

The exceptional group G2 has dimension 14, rank 2, and a trivial center. Its Langlands dual is again G2, but the duality exchanges the long and short roots. The root system of type G2 has 12 roots: 6 long roots forming an A2 subsystem and 6 short roots forming another A2 subsystem. The moduli stack Bun G2 is connected. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence at the critical level (k = −4) is expected to be an equivalence D b hol (D‑mod(Bun G2)) ≃ QCoh(LocSys G2) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution. The Hecke functors are labeled by the fundamental representations of G2. The fundamental representations have dimensions 7 (ω₁, the minimal representation) and 14 (ω₂, the adjoint). The 7‑dimensional representation is minuscule for the short root system: its weights are the 6 short roots plus 0. The 14‑dimensional adjoint representation decomposes under the long root A2 as 8 ⊕ 3 ⊕ 3. The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the 7‑dimensional representation is computed by the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the G2 Hitchin system. The Hitchin base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6; the generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 7(g−1). The spectral curve is a 7‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 7‑dimensional representation. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical levels involves the quantum group U q (𝔤₂) with q = exp(πi/(k+4)). The quantum group U q (𝔤₂) has a non‑trivial R‑matrix and a ribbon element. The representation theory of U q (𝔤₂) at roots of unity controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for G2‑connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety, whose Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of G2. The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for G2 studies the interplay between the long and short root systems under the Langlands duality. The embedding of the long root A2 into G2 induces a functor between the category of D‑modules on Bun G2 and the category of D‑modules on Bun SL(3), which intertwines the Hecke actions. The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the 7‑dimensional representation on the G2 side corresponds to the categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the symmetric square of the standard representation of SL(3) on the SL(3) side, up to a normalization factor. The Verlinde formula for G2 computes the dimension of the space of conformal blocks as a sum over the modular S‑matrix of the affine Kac–Moody algebra 𝔤̂₂. The modular S‑matrix for G2 at level k has entries given by the Kac–Peterson formula involving the Weyl group of order 12. The fusion rules for the integrable representations of 𝔤̂₂ at level k are governed by the Verlinde algebra, which is isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of the category of D‑modules on Bun G2 twisted by the line bundle of level k.

  • Confusing the component group of Bun G with the center of the dual group L G; they are dual but not canonically identified without a pinning.
  • Assuming the Whittaker category on Bun G captures the whole derived category for non‑simply connected groups; it only captures the trivial topological component.
  • Treating the critical level quantization parameter ℏ as a root of unity; it is generic unless k + h∨ is a rational number with denominator dividing the order of the center.
  • Neglecting that the Hitchin fibers for non‑simply connected groups are disconnected, and that the spectral curves for different components may have different genera.
  • Misidentifying the Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter with the affine Kac–Moody level k; the relation is ℏ = 1/(k + h∨).
  • Overlooking that the irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for exceptional groups involves Stokes matrices valued in the dual group, not merely formal monodromy.
  • Assuming the geometric Satake equivalence for non‑simply connected groups is a simple restriction of the simply connected case; the monoidal structure acquires a non‑trivial associator from the component group.

The Categorical Geometric Langlands Correspondence for G2 via the Whittaker Category

The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for the simply connected group G2 at the critical level admits a formulation through the Whittaker category, which provides a bridge between the Hecke-theoretic and the spectral sides. For G2, the moduli stack BunG2 is connected because G2 has trivial center and is simply connected. The Whittaker category Whit(BunG2) is defined as the category of D-modules on BunG2 equipped with a Whittaker coefficient along the maximal unipotent subgroup U ⊂ G2. The Hecke functors HV for representations V of the Langlands dual group LG2 = G2 act on this category, and the categorical geometric Langlands equivalence predicts an equivalence Whit(BunG2) ≃ QCoh(LocSysG2)Wh, where the right side is the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on the stack of G2-local systems with a Whittaker condition at a fixed point.

The fundamental representations of G2 are the 7-dimensional representation ω1 (minuscule for the short root system) and the 14-dimensional adjoint representation ω2. The Hecke functor Hω₁ corresponds under the equivalence to the operation of tensoring with the universal local system in the 7-dimensional representation. The categorical trace of Hω₁ computes the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the G2 Hitchin system, whose base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2 and 6. The generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 7(g−1), and the spectral curve is a 7-fold cover of the base curve X defined by the characteristic polynomial in the 7-dimensional representation. The Whittaker category formulation is particularly powerful because it isolates the component of the correspondence that is expected to be an equivalence without the complications of disconnected components that arise for non-simply connected groups.

The proof strategy for G2 follows the general framework of Arinkin-Gaitsgory but requires specific analysis of the G2 root system. The long roots form an A2 subsystem, and the short roots form another A2 subsystem. The embedding of the long root A2 into G2 induces a functor between the Whittaker category for G2 and the Whittaker category for SL(3), which intertwines the Hecke actions. This functor is essential for reducing the G2 case to the better-understood SL(3) case. The categorical trace of Hω₁ on the G2 side corresponds to the categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the symmetric square of the standard representation of SL(3) on the SL(3) side, up to a normalization factor determined by the embedding index. The Verlinde formula for G2 computes the dimension of the space of conformal blocks as a sum over the modular S-matrix of the affine Kac–Moody algebra ĝ2 at level k, with the Weyl group of order 12 acting on the weight lattice.

The Quantum Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Exceptional Group E6

The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence for the exceptional group E6 at non-critical levels relates the category of D-modules on the moduli stack BunE6 twisted by a line bundle of level k to the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on the moduli stack of LE6-local systems with a quantum parameter q = exp(πi/(k+12)), where 12 is the dual Coxeter number h∨ of E6. The simply connected group E6 has center Z/3Z, and its Langlands dual is the adjoint group LE6 = E6ad with fundamental group Z/3Z. The moduli stack BunE6 has three connected components indexed by the center Z/3Z, while the stack of LE6-local systems LocSysLE6 has components indexed by the fundamental group Z/3Z. The quantum geometric Langlands equivalence is expected to be a family of equivalences Dk-mod(BunE6) ≃ QCohq(LocSysLE6) parameterized by the level k, where Dk denotes the sheaf of twisted differential operators.

The fundamental representations of E6 have dimensions 27 (the minimal representation) and 78 (the adjoint representation). The 27-dimensional representation is minuscule, and its weights are the 27 lines on a cubic surface. The Hecke functors H27 and H78 generate the Hecke category. The categorical trace of H27 computes the quantum Hitchin Hamiltonian for the E6 Hitchin system, whose base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 5, 6, 8, 9, 12. The generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 36(g−1). The spectral curve is a 27-fold cover of the base curve X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 27-dimensional representation. The quantum group Uq(𝔢6) at q = exp(πi/(k+12)) controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory. The representation theory of Uq(𝔢6) at roots of unity is governed by the Verlinde algebra, which is isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of the category of D-modules on BunE6 twisted by the line bundle of level k.

The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E6-connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind-sheaf on the wild character variety, whose Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of LE6. The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for E6 studies the interplay between the component group of BunE6 (Z/3Z) and the center of LE6 (also Z/3Z). The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence at the critical level (k = −12) is expected to be an equivalence Dbhol(D-mod(BunE6)) ≃ QCoh(LocSysLE6) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution. The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter ℏ is related to the affine Kac–Moody level by ℏ = 1/(k+12). The center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at non-critical levels is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of LE6-opers, and this isomorphism is a key ingredient in the construction of the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence.

Worked Example: Computing the Quantum Parameter for E6
Let k = −6 be the level for the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence for E6. Compute the quantum parameter q and determine whether it is a root of unity. If it is a root of unity, find its order.
The dual Coxeter number of E6 is h∨ = 12. The quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(k + h∨)) = exp(πi/(−6 + 12)) = exp(πi/6). This is a primitive 12th root of unity because exp(πi/6) has order 12: (exp(πi/6))12 = exp(2πi) = 1, and no smaller positive power gives 1. At this root of unity, the quantum group Uq(𝔢6) has a finite-dimensional representation theory controlled by the Verlinde algebra, and the category of D-modules on BunE6 twisted by level k = −6 is expected to be equivalent to the category of representations of the corresponding quantum group at this root of unity.
Exam Strategy: Quantum Parameter and Root of Unity
When a problem gives a level k for a simply connected group G, always compute q = exp(πi/(k + h∨)) where h∨ is the dual Coxeter number. Check if k + h∨ is rational; if so, q is a root of unity. The order is the denominator of (k + h∨) in lowest terms, multiplied by 2 if the numerator is odd. For E6, h∨ = 12; for E7, h∨ = 18; for E8, h∨ = 30. Memorize these three numbers — they appear repeatedly in quantum geometric Langlands problems for exceptional groups.

The Theta Correspondence for D-modules on the Moduli Stack of G2-Bundles

The theta correspondence for D-modules on the moduli stack of G2-bundles arises from the exceptional isomorphism between the split form of G2 and the automorphism group of the split octonions, which provides a dual pair (G2, SO(7)) inside the split form of E7. The 7-dimensional fundamental representation of G2 is the spin representation of SO(7) restricted to G2, and this embedding G2 ⊂ SO(7) induces a theta lifting functor between the category of D-modules on BunG2 and the category of D-modules on BunSO(7). The moduli stack BunSO(7) parametrizes orthogonal bundles of rank 7 with trivial determinant, and its connected components are indexed by the second Stiefel–Whitney class w2 ∈ H2(X, Z/2Z). The theta lifting functor Θ: D-mod(BunG2) → D-mod(BunSO(7)) is defined as the integral transform with kernel given by the D-module of sections of the theta line bundle on the correspondence space.

The theta correspondence intertwines the Hecke actions: the Hecke functor for the 7-dimensional representation of G2 corresponds to the Hecke functor for the standard representation of SO(7) under the theta lift. The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the 7-dimensional representation of G2 computes the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the G2 Hitchin system, while the categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the standard representation of SO(7) computes the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the SO(7) Hitchin system. The theta correspondence identifies these two Hamiltonians, providing a geometric proof of the equality of the corresponding quantum integrable systems. The spectral curve for the G2 Hitchin system is a 7-fold cover of X, while the spectral curve for the SO(7) Hitchin system is a 7-fold cover given by the characteristic polynomial of the Higgs field in the standard representation. The theta correspondence identifies these two spectral curves as the same curve in the total space of the cotangent bundle of X.

The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for G2-connections with irregular singularities can be studied via the theta correspondence by relating them to SO(7)-connections. The Stokes matrices for G2-connections are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of G2, while the Stokes matrices for SO(7)-connections are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of SO(7). The embedding G2 ⊂ SO(7) identifies the maximal unipotent subgroup of G2 with a subgroup of the maximal unipotent subgroup of SO(7). The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for G2 then follows from the wild geometric Langlands correspondence for SO(7) via the theta lift. The Verlinde formula for G2 at level k can be derived from the Verlinde formula for SO(7) at the corresponding level by restricting the sum over the modular S-matrix to the G2-invariant subspace. The modular S-matrix for G2 at level k has entries given by the Kac–Peterson formula involving the Weyl group of order 12, while the modular S-matrix for SO(7) involves the Weyl group of order 23·3! = 48.

The Categorical Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Exceptional Group E7 via the Whittaker Category

The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for the simply connected group E7 at the critical level admits a Whittaker category formulation analogous to the G2 case but with richer structure due to the larger rank and the non-trivial center Z/2Z. The moduli stack BunE7 has two connected components indexed by the center Z/2Z, while the Langlands dual group LE7 = E7ad has fundamental group Z/2Z. The Whittaker category Whit(BunE7) is defined as the category of D-modules on the neutral component BunE70 equipped with a Whittaker coefficient along the maximal unipotent subgroup U ⊂ E7. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence predicts an equivalence Whit(BunE7) ≃ QCoh(LocSysLE7)Wh, where the right side is the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on the stack of LE7-local systems with a Whittaker condition, supported on the component corresponding to the trivial element of the fundamental group.

The fundamental representations of E7 have dimensions 56 (the minimal representation, which is symplectic) and 133 (the adjoint representation). The 56-dimensional representation is minuscule, and its weights correspond to the 28 bitangents of a plane quartic curve. The Hecke functors H56 and H133 generate the Hecke category. The categorical trace of H56 computes the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the E7 Hitchin system, whose base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 18. The generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 63(g−1). The spectral curve is a 56-fold cover of the base curve X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 56-dimensional representation. The embedding of the long root subsystem (which is of type E6) into E7 induces a functor between the Whittaker category for E7 and the Whittaker category for E6, which intertwines the Hecke actions. This functor is essential for reducing the E7 case to the E6 case.

The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence for E7 at non-critical levels involves the quantum group Uq(𝔢7) with q = exp(πi/(k+18)), where 18 is the dual Coxeter number h∨ of E7. The representation theory of Uq(𝔢7) at roots of unity controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E7-connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind-sheaf on the wild character variety, whose Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of LE7. The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for E7 studies the interplay between the component group of BunE7 (Z/2Z) and the center of LE7 (also Z/2Z). The categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the 56-dimensional representation on the E7 side corresponds to the categorical trace of the Hecke functor for the appropriate representation on the E6 side under the embedding functor, up to a normalization factor determined by the embedding index.

The Quantum Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Exceptional Group E8

The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence for the exceptional group E8 is unique among the exceptional groups because E8 is simply connected, has trivial center, and is self-dual: LE8 = E8. The moduli stack BunE8 is connected, and the stack of E8-local systems LocSysE8 is also connected. The dual Coxeter number is h∨ = 30, so the quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(k+30)). The fundamental representation of E8 is the 248-dimensional adjoint representation, which is also the minimal representation. There are no minuscule representations for E8. The Hecke category is generated by the Hecke functor H248 for the adjoint representation. The categorical trace of H248 computes the Hitchin Hamiltonian for the E8 Hitchin system, whose base is generated by invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 8, 12, 14, 18, 20, 24, 30. The generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 120(g−1). The spectral curve is a 248-fold cover of the base curve X given by the characteristic polynomial in the adjoint representation.

The quantum group Uq(𝔢8) at q = exp(πi/(k+30)) controls the fusion rules of the corresponding conformal field theory. The representation theory of Uq(𝔢8) at roots of unity is governed by the Verlinde algebra, which is isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of the category of D-modules on BunE8 twisted by the line bundle of level k. The modular S-matrix for E8 at level k has entries given by the Kac–Peterson formula involving the Weyl group of order 696,729,600. The fusion rules for the integrable representations of ê8 at level k are determined by the Verlinde formula. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E8-connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind-sheaf on the wild character variety, whose Stokes matrices are elements of the maximal unipotent subgroup of E8. The wild geometric Langlands correspondence for E8 is particularly clean because there is no component group to complicate the picture: both BunE8 and LocSysE8 are connected.

The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence at the critical level (k = −30) is expected to be an equivalence Dbhol(D-mod(BunE8)) ≃ QCoh(LocSysE8) intertwining the Hecke action with convolution. The Beilinson–Drinfeld quantization parameter ℏ is related to the affine Kac–Moody level by ℏ = 1/(k+30). The center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at non-critical levels is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of E8-opers. The E8 Hitchin system is the most complicated of the exceptional Hitchin systems, and its spectral curves have the highest degree covers. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence for E8 at non-critical levels provides a family of equivalences Dk-mod(BunE8) ≃ QCohq(LocSysE8) that specializes at the critical level to the categorical geometric Langlands equivalence. The Verlinde formula for E8 computes the dimension of the space of conformal blocks as a sum over the modular S-matrix of the affine Kac–Moody algebra ê8 at level k.

  • Confusing the dual Coxeter numbers of exceptional groups: E6 has h∨ = 12, E7 has h∨ = 18, E8 has h∨ = 30. G2 has h∨ = 4, F4 has h∨ = 9. These numbers determine the critical level k = −h∨ and the quantum parameter q = exp(πi/(k+h∨)).
  • Assuming the Whittaker category for a simply connected group captures the whole derived category of D-modules on BunG; it only captures the neutral component. For groups with non-trivial center (E6, E7), the other components require separate treatment.
  • Treating the quantum parameter q as generic when k + h∨ is rational; q is a root of unity exactly when k + h∨ ∈ Q, and the order of q is the denominator of k + h∨ in lowest terms (times 2 if the numerator is odd).
  • Neglecting that the theta correspondence for G2 ⊂ SO(7) identifies the 7-dimensional representation of G2 with the standard representation of SO(7), but the adjoint representation of G2 (14-dimensional) corresponds to the adjoint representation of SO(7) (21-dimensional) only after decomposition.
  • Misidentifying the Langlands dual of E6: the simply connected E6 has dual the adjoint E6ad with fundamental group Z/3Z, not the simply connected E6 again. The center of E6 is Z/3Z, and the fundamental group of E6ad is Z/3Z.
  • Overlooking that E8 has no minuscule representations; the minimal representation is the 248-dimensional adjoint representation. This means the Hecke category for E8 is generated by a single Hecke functor H248, unlike G2, E6, E7 which have minuscule representations giving smaller Hecke functors.
  • Assuming the geometric Satake equivalence for E8 is trivial because E8 is self-dual; the monoidal structure on the spherical Hecke category for E8 is non-trivial and encodes the tensor product of representations of E8, which is highly non-trivial due to the large dimension of the adjoint representation.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G2‑Bundles and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence

The exceptional group G2, of dimension 14 and dual Coxeter number 4, occupies a distinguished place in the geometric Langlands program because it is the smallest exceptional group and its Langlands dual is again G2. The moduli stack BunG2 of G2‑bundles on a smooth projective curve X carries a natural family of twisted D‑modules Dκ‑mod(BunG2) parametrized by a level κ ∈ ℂ. At the critical level κ = −4 the center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra ĝ2 is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of G2‑opers on X. The categorical geometric Langlands conjecture predicts an equivalence

D−4‑modhol(BunG2) ≃ QCoh(LocSysG2)

intertwining the Hecke functors for the three fundamental representations (the 7‑dimensional standard representation, the 14‑dimensional adjoint, and the 27‑dimensional representation) with convolution by the corresponding local systems on LocSysG2. The 7‑dimensional representation is minuscule, so its Hecke functor H7 generates the spherical Hecke category. The 14‑dimensional adjoint representation gives a Hecke functor H14 whose categorical trace computes the Hitchin Hamiltonians for the G2 Hitchin system. The base of the Hitchin fibration is the affine space of invariant polynomials of degrees 2 and 6; the generic fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 3(g−1). The spectral curve is a 7‑fold cover of X defined by the characteristic polynomial in the standard representation. At non‑critical levels κ ≠ −4 the quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(κ+4)). The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence provides a family of equivalences Dκ‑mod(BunG2) ≃ QCohq(LocSysG2) that specialize at κ = −4 to the categorical equivalence. The Verlinde formula for G2 at level κ computes the dimension of the space of conformal blocks as a sum over the modular S‑matrix of ĝ2 at level κ. The fusion rules of the integrable representations of ĝ2 at roots of unity are governed by the Verlinde algebra, which is isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of the category of twisted D‑modules on BunG2. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for G2‑connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety; the Stokes matrices lie in the maximal unipotent subgroup of G2. Because the center of G2 is trivial, both BunG2 and LocSysG2 are connected, simplifying the wild geometric Langlands correspondence.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of F4‑Bundles and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence

The exceptional group F4 has dimension 52, dual Coxeter number 9, and is self‑dual. Its moduli stack BunF4 of F4‑bundles on a curve X supports twisted D‑modules Dκ‑mod(BunF4) with level κ. The critical level is κ = −9. The center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra f̂4 at the critical level is the algebra of functions on the space of F4‑opers. F4 possesses two minuscule representations: the 26‑dimensional representation (the smallest non‑trivial representation) and the 52‑dimensional adjoint representation. The Hecke category is therefore generated by two Hecke functors H26 and H52. The categorical trace of H26 yields the Hitchin Hamiltonians for the F4 Hitchin system, whose base is the affine space of invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 6, 8, 12. The generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 12(g−1). The spectral curve is a 26‑fold cover of X given by the characteristic polynomial in the 26‑dimensional representation. At non‑critical levels the quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(κ+9)). The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence gives equivalences Dκ‑mod(BunF4) ≃ QCohq(LocSysF4). The Verlinde formula for F4 involves the modular S‑matrix of f̂4 at level κ; the fusion rules are encoded in the Verlinde algebra, isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of twisted D‑modules. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for F4‑connections with irregular singularities produces an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety; Stokes matrices belong to the maximal unipotent subgroup of F4. The center of F4 is trivial, so BunF4 and LocSysF4 are connected, and the wild geometric Langlands correspondence has no component‑group complications.

Examiner's trap
A question asks: “For the group F4, which representation gives the Hecke functor that generates the spherical Hecke category?” A common mistake is to answer “the adjoint representation (52‑dimensional)”. The correct answer is the 26‑dimensional minuscule representation, because minuscule representations generate the spherical Hecke category; the adjoint representation is not minuscule.
The spherical Hecke category for a reductive group G is generated by Hecke functors attached to minuscule representations. For F4 the only minuscule representation is the 26‑dimensional one (the adjoint 52‑dimensional representation is not minuscule). Therefore the Hecke functor H26 generates the spherical Hecke category. The adjoint Hecke functor H52 provides additional structure but does not generate the category alone.
Exam‑day strategy
Memorize the minuscule representations for each exceptional group: G2 has the 7‑dimensional standard representation; F4 has the 26‑dimensional representation; E6 has the 27‑dimensional representation; E7 has the 56‑dimensional representation; E8 has none. Knowing these instantly identifies the generator of the spherical Hecke category and avoids the trap of picking the adjoint representation.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of E6‑Bundles and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for Non‑Simply Connected Forms

The simply connected group E6 has center ℤ/3ℤ and dual Coxeter number 12. Its Langlands dual is the adjoint form E6ad with fundamental group ℤ/3ℤ. The moduli stacks BunE6 and BunE6ad have three connected components each, indexed by the center or fundamental group. Twisted D‑modules Dκ‑mod(BunE6) at level κ decompose according to these components. The critical level is κ = −12. The center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra ê6 at the critical level is the algebra of functions on the space of E6‑opers. E6 possesses two minuscule representations: the 27‑dimensional representation and its dual 27̅. The Hecke category for the simply connected form is generated by the Hecke functors H27 and H27̅. For the adjoint form the minuscule representations are absent; the Hecke category is generated by the Hecke functor for the 78‑dimensional adjoint representation. The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence at the critical level is expected to be an equivalence between the derived category of holonomic D‑modules on the neutral component of BunE6 and the category of quasi‑coherent sheaves on the neutral component of LocSysE6ad, intertwining Hecke actions with convolution. The other components require separate treatment via the Whittaker category or by twisting with line bundles representing characters of the center. At non‑critical levels the quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(κ+12)). The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence provides a family of equivalences for each component. The Verlinde formula for E6 at level κ involves the modular S‑matrix of ê6; the fusion rules are governed by the Verlinde algebra, isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of twisted D‑modules on the neutral component. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E6‑connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety; Stokes matrices lie in the maximal unipotent subgroup of E6. The component group ℤ/3ℤ introduces additional monodromy data that must be matched on both sides of the wild geometric Langlands correspondence.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of E7‑Bundles and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for Non‑Simply Connected Forms

The simply connected group E7 has center ℤ/2ℤ and dual Coxeter number 18. Its Langlands dual is the adjoint form E7ad with fundamental group ℤ/2ℤ. The moduli stacks BunE7 and BunE7ad each have two connected components. Twisted D‑modules Dκ‑mod(BunE7) at level κ split accordingly. The critical level is κ = −18. The center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra ê7 at the critical level is the algebra of functions on the space of E7‑opers. E7 has a single minuscule representation: the 56‑dimensional representation. The Hecke category for the simply connected form is generated by the Hecke functor H56. For the adjoint form there are no minuscule representations; the Hecke category is generated by the Hecke functor for the 133‑dimensional adjoint representation. The categorical geometric Langlands correspondence at the critical level is expected to give an equivalence between the derived category of holonomic D‑modules on the neutral component of BunE7 and QCoh on the neutral component of LocSysE7ad. The non‑neutral components are accessed by twisting with the non‑trivial character of the center ℤ/2ℤ. At non‑critical levels the quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(κ+18)). The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence yields equivalences for each component. The Verlinde formula for E7 uses the modular S‑matrix of ê7; the fusion rules are encoded in the Verlinde algebra, isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of twisted D‑modules on the neutral component. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E7‑connections with irregular singularities produces an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety; Stokes matrices belong to the maximal unipotent subgroup of E7. The ℤ/2ℤ component group introduces a sign ambiguity in the wild geometric Langlands correspondence that must be resolved by a choice of Whittaker normalization.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of E8‑Bundles and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence at the Critical Level

The exceptional group E8 has dimension 248, dual Coxeter number 30, trivial center, and is self‑dual. Its moduli stack BunE8 is connected. Twisted D‑modules Dκ‑mod(BunE8) are parametrized by a level κ. The critical level is κ = −30. The center of the affine Kac–Moody algebra ê8 at the critical level is the algebra of functions on the space of E8‑opers. E8 has no minuscule representations; the minimal representation is the 248‑dimensional adjoint representation. Consequently the spherical Hecke category is generated by a single Hecke functor H248. The categorical trace of H248 computes the Hitchin Hamiltonians for the E8 Hitchin system, whose base is the affine space of invariant polynomials of degrees 2, 8, 12, 14, 18, 20, 24, 30. The generic Hitchin fiber is an abelian variety of dimension 120(g−1). The spectral curve is a 248‑fold cover of X defined by the characteristic polynomial in the adjoint representation. At non‑critical levels the quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(κ+30)). The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence provides a family of equivalences Dκ‑mod(BunE8) ≃ QCohq(LocSysE8) that specialize at κ = −30 to the categorical geometric Langlands equivalence D−30‑modhol(BunE8) ≃ QCoh(LocSysE8). The Verlinde formula for E8 involves the modular S‑matrix of ê8 at level κ; the Weyl group of E8 has order 696,729,600. The fusion rules of the integrable representations of ê8 at roots of unity are governed by the Verlinde algebra, isomorphic to the Grothendieck ring of twisted D‑modules on BunE8. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for E8‑connections with irregular singularities attaches an enhanced ind‑sheaf on the wild character variety; Stokes matrices lie in the maximal unipotent subgroup of E8. Because both BunE8 and LocSysE8 are connected, the wild geometric Langlands correspondence for E8 is particularly clean.

  • Confusing the dual Coxeter numbers of exceptional groups: E6 has h∨ = 12, E7 has h∨ = 18, E8 has h∨ = 30. G2 has h∨ = 4, F4 has h∨ = 9. These numbers determine the critical level k = −h∨ and the quantum parameter q = exp(πi/(k+h∨)).
  • Assuming the Whittaker category for a simply connected group captures the whole derived category of D‑modules on BunG; it only captures the neutral component. For groups with non‑trivial center (E6, E7), the other components require separate treatment.
  • Treating the quantum parameter q as generic when k + h∨ is rational; q is a root of unity exactly when k + h∨ ∈ ℚ, and the order of q is the denominator of k + h∨ in lowest terms (times 2 if the numerator is odd).
  • Neglecting that the theta correspondence for G2 ⊂ SO(7) identifies the 7‑dimensional representation of G2 with the standard representation of SO(7), but the adjoint representation of G2 (14‑dimensional) corresponds to the adjoint representation of SO(7) (21‑dimensional) only after decomposition.
  • Misidentifying the Langlands dual of E6: the simply connected E6 has dual the adjoint E6ad with fundamental group ℤ/3ℤ, not the simply connected E6 again. The center of E6 is ℤ/3ℤ, and the fundamental group of E6ad is ℤ/3ℤ.
  • Overlooking that E8 has no minuscule representations; the minimal representation is the 248‑dimensional adjoint representation. This means the Hecke category for E8 is generated by a single Hecke functor H248, unlike G2, E6, E7 which have minuscule representations giving smaller Hecke functors.
  • Assuming the geometric Satake equivalence for E8 is trivial because E8 is self‑dual; the monoidal structure on the spherical Hecke category for E8 is non‑trivial and encodes the tensor product of representations of E8, which is highly non‑trivial due to the large dimension of the adjoint representation.

The Kapustin–Witten Topological Twist and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence via D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles

The Kapustin–Witten topological twist of four‑dimensional N = 4 super Yang–Mills theory with gauge group G on a four‑manifold M = Σ × C, where Σ is a Riemann surface and C is a two‑dimensional cigar, produces a topological field theory whose boundary conditions on Σ are governed by the geometric Langlands correspondence. The twist depends on a complex parameter t ∈ ℂℙ¹; at t = 0, ∞ one recovers the A‑model and B‑model topological sigma models with target the Hitchin moduli space MH(G, Σ). For generic t the theory is a one‑parameter family interpolating between them. The space of boundary conditions of type B at t = 0 is the derived category of coherent sheaves on the moduli stack LocSysG(Σ) of G‑local systems, while the space of boundary conditions of type A at t = ∞ is the Fukaya category of MH(G, Σ). The geometric Langlands correspondence is the equivalence of these two categories induced by the S‑duality of the underlying gauge theory. In the algebraic formulation, the B‑model boundary conditions are replaced by the derived category of holonomic D‑modules on the moduli stack BunG(Σ) of G‑bundles, and the equivalence becomes the categorical geometric Langlands correspondence D‑modhol(BunG) ≃ QCoh(LocSysG). The parameter t is related to the level κ of the twisted D‑modules by t = i(κ + h∨)−1/2, where h∨ is the dual Coxeter number. At the critical level κ = −h∨ one has t = ∞, corresponding to the classical geometric Langlands correspondence. The Kapustin–Witten equations on Σ × ℝ≥0 with Nahm pole boundary conditions at the origin produce the Hecke eigensheaf property: a D‑module M on BunG is a Hecke eigensheaf with eigenvalue a local system E if the solution to the Kapustin–Witten equations with boundary condition determined by M at infinity and the Nahm pole at zero has monodromy E. This physical derivation explains the appearance of the Hitchin system, the spectral curve, and the Langlands dual group G∨ as the gauge group of the dual theory.

D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles with Surface Defects and the 4d‑2d Correspondence

Surface defects in the Kapustin–Witten theory are codimension‑two operators supported on Σ × {point} ⊂ Σ × C. They are labelled by a pair (H, ρ) where H ⊂ G is a Levi subgroup and ρ is a representation of the Langlands dual H∨. In the B‑model at t = 0, a surface defect modifies the category of boundary conditions by tensoring with the category of representations of H∨, which on the LocSys side corresponds to the operation of restricting a G‑local system to an H‑local system and then inducing back via ρ. On the A‑model side at t = ∞, the defect creates a Lagrangian correspondence in the Hitchin moduli space given by the Hecke correspondence for the subgroup H. In the algebraic formulation with D‑modules on BunG, a surface defect of type (H, ρ) induces a functor between categories of D‑modules on BunG and BunH given by the composition of the pullback along the projection BunH → BunG (forgetting the H‑reduction) and the pushforward twisted by the D‑module on BunH corresponding to ρ under the geometric Satake equivalence. This functor intertwines the Hecke functors for G and H, and its categorical trace computes the transfer of Hitchin Hamiltonians from G to H. The 4d‑2d correspondence states that the effective theory on the surface defect is a two‑dimensional N = (2,2) supersymmetric gauge theory with gauge group H and matter content determined by ρ. The chiral ring of this 2d theory is the quantum cohomology of the flag variety G/H, and its category of boundary conditions is the derived category of D‑modules on G/H. The geometric Langlands correspondence with surface defects therefore relates the category of D‑modules on BunG with a defect to the category of D‑modules on BunH twisted by the quantum cohomology of G/H. For H = T a maximal torus and ρ the trivial representation, the defect is the Gukov–Witten defect, and the 2d theory is the G/T sigma model whose chiral ring is the quantum cohomology of the full flag variety. The categorical trace of the corresponding functor on D‑mod(BunG) recovers the quantum Hitchin Hamiltonians, which are the eigenvalues of the quantum G‑opers.

Worked Example: Surface Defect Functor for G = SL(2), H = T
Let G = SL(2), H = T ≃ ℂ× the diagonal torus, and ρ the trivial representation. Describe the surface defect functor F: D-mod(Bun_SL(2)) → D-mod(Bun_T) and compute its effect on the Hecke eigensheaf corresponding to a rank‑2 local system E with determinant 1.
The moduli stack Bun_T is the Picard stack Pic(Σ) × Pic(Σ) parametrising pairs of line bundles (L₁, L₂). The projection p: Bun_T → Bun_SL(2) sends (L₁, L₂) to the rank‑2 bundle L₁ ⊕ L₂ with the induced SL(2)‑structure (det = L₁ ⊗ L₂ ≃ O). The surface defect functor is F(M) = p*(M) ⊗ DBun_T, where DBun_T is the trivial D‑module on Bun_T (since ρ is trivial). For a Hecke eigensheaf M_E with eigenvalue E, the Hecke property H_V(M_E) ≃ M_E ⊗ V(E) for V the standard representation implies that p*(M_E) is a Hecke eigensheaf for the torus with eigenvalue the restriction of E to T. The local system E splits as L ⊕ L⁻¹ on the spectral cover; its restriction to T is the pair (L, L⁻¹). Hence F(M_E) is the D‑module on Pic(Σ) × Pic(Σ) supported on the locus where the product of the two line bundles is trivial, with monodromy given by (L, L⁻¹). This is precisely the D‑module corresponding to the abelianised local system under the abelian geometric Langlands correspondence.
Exam Strategy: Surface Defects and Hecke Functors
When a question asks about surface defects, identify the Levi subgroup H and the representation ρ. The functor is always a composition of pullback along Bun_H → Bun_G and a twist by the D‑module on Bun_H coming from ρ via geometric Satake. For the Gukov–Witten defect (H = T, ρ = trivial), the functor is simply pullback to the torus. The categorical trace of this functor gives the quantum Hitchin Hamiltonians; remember that the quantum parameter is q = exp(πi/(κ + h∨)). If the problem mentions Nahm pole boundary conditions, it is referring to the Kapustin–Witten derivation of the Hecke eigensheaf property, not the algebraic definition.

The Theory of D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Geometric Langlands Correspondence with Line Operators and the Verlinde Line Operators

Line operators in the Kapustin–Witten theory are codimension‑three operators supported on a curve C ⊂ Σ × {point}. They are classified by representations of the quantum group Uq(g) at q = exp(πi/(κ + h∨)). In the B‑model at t = 0, a line operator labelled by a representation V of Uq(g) acts on the category QCoh(LocSysG) by tensoring with the vector bundle on LocSysG whose fibre at a local system E is the space of conformal blocks of the Uq(g)‑WZW model with insertion V at the point. On the A‑model side at t = ∞, the line operator creates a Lagrangian brane in the Hitchin moduli space given by the spectral curve with a Wilson line in representation V. In the algebraic formulation, line operators act on D‑mod(BunG) by the Hecke functors HV at the critical level κ = −h∨, and by their quantum deformations at non‑critical levels. The Verlinde line operators are the line operators corresponding to the integrable representations of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at level k = κ + h∨. Their fusion rules are governed by the Verlinde formula, which computes the dimension of the space of conformal blocks. The categorical trace of the Verlinde line operator HV on a Hecke eigensheaf ME is the character of the representation V evaluated on the monodromy of E around the insertion point. For G = SL(2), the Verlinde line operators are labelled by spins j = 0, 1/2, …, k/2, and their fusion is the truncated tensor product of Uq(sl₂) representations. The space of conformal blocks for n insertions on a genus g curve is the space of coinvariants of the tensor product of the corresponding representations under the action of the affine Lie algebra. The geometric Langlands correspondence with line operators states that the category of D‑modules on BunG with line operator insertions is equivalent to the category of quasi‑coherent sheaves on LocSysG with the corresponding vector bundles. This equivalence is compatible with the fusion of line operators, which corresponds to the tensor product of representations of Uq(g). The Verlinde algebra, which is the Grothendieck ring of the fusion category, is isomorphic to the ring of functions on the space of G‑opers, and the Verlinde formula is the statement that the categorical trace of the identity functor on the category of D‑modules with n insertions equals the dimension of the space of conformal blocks.

D‑modules on the Moduli Stack of G‑Bundles for the Geometric Langlands Correspondence with the Fock–Goncharov Cluster Structure and the Quantum Dilogarithm

The moduli space LocSysG(Σ) of G‑local systems on a punctured surface Σ carries a natural cluster Poisson structure discovered by Fock and Goncharov. The cluster coordinates are associated to an ideal triangulation of Σ and take values in the Langlands dual group G∨. The cluster mutations correspond to flips of the triangulation and are given by the quantum dilogarithm function Φq(x) = ∏n≥0 (1 + q2n+1x)−1 for |q| < 1. In the geometric Langlands correspondence, the cluster structure on LocSysG is mirrored by a cluster structure on the category of D‑modules on BunG. The cluster variables on the D‑module side are the eigenvalues of the quantum Hitchin Hamiltonians, which are the quantum G‑opers. The quantum dilogarithm appears as the kernel of the integral transform that implements the cluster mutation on the category of D‑modules. For G = SL(2), the cluster coordinates on LocSysSL(2) are the trace functions of the monodromy along the edges of the triangulation. The quantum mutation is the operator Φq(X) where X is the quantum cluster variable satisfying XY = q²YX. The category of D‑modules on BunSL(2) with the cluster structure is equivalent to the category of modules over the quantum cluster algebra, which is generated by the quantum cluster variables with relations given by the quantum exchange relations. The quantum dilogarithm identity Φq(X)Φq(Y) = Φq(Y)Φq(q⁻¹XY)Φq(X) is the pentagon identity for the quantum mutation, and it ensures the associativity of the fusion of line operators.
